PREVIOUSLY ADDED KYMEX AI EXPANSION SECTIONS — PRESERVED
================================================================================

PART 7: KYMEX AI UNIVERSAL OPERATING PLATFORM — EXPANDED IMPLEMENTATION
SPECIFICATION
========================================================================================
This section is an additive implementation layer for the two source
documents above. It expands the platform into a unified AI workspace
spanning conversation, coding, research, application building,
automation, media, data, documents, browser agents, integrations, and
enterprise operations. Provider names are treated as connector targets;
actual availability depends on authorized integrations, current APIs,
product terms, and backend configuration.

01. MASTER PRODUCT DIRECTIVE

01.001 Treat KYMEX AI as a universal AI operating workspace, not merely
a chatbot. 01.002 Preserve the existing KYMEX AI source documents as
foundational requirements. 01.003 Add the new implementation as an
additive specification; do not silently remove existing capabilities.
01.004 Do not claim a provider, integration, model, tool, or action is
available unless it is configured and operational. 01.005 Every provider
integration must be implemented behind a provider adapter interface.
01.006 Every capability must be discoverable from natural language and
from explicit UI entry points. 01.007 Every consequential external
action must respect the configured approval policy. 01.008 Every
privileged operation must be authorized server-side, never only by
client UI. 01.009 Every asynchronous job must expose a durable job state
and recoverable status. 01.010 Every generated artifact must receive a
persistent artifact identifier. 01.011 Every artifact must preserve
provenance including model, provider, tool, inputs, timestamps, and
status where policy permits. 01.012 Every tool invocation must be
auditable without exposing secrets. 01.013 Every provider failure must
degrade gracefully to a configured fallback. 01.014 Every fallback must
preserve conversation context and user intent. 01.015 Never fabricate
successful execution when a tool call failed or was not attempted.
01.016 Show the user what was actually completed, what is pending, and
what requires approval. 01.017 Support both automatic orchestration and
explicit user-selected tools. 01.018 Allow users to override an
automatic model or tool choice when their plan and permissions permit.
01.019 Keep the interface coherent even when multiple providers
participate in one task. 01.020 Make the system extensible so newly
released providers can be added without redesigning the application.
01.021 Treat the provider catalog as configuration data rather than
hardcoded UI logic. 01.022 Treat capabilities as modules with declared
requirements, inputs, outputs, permissions, and costs. 01.023 Treat
agents as reusable configurations of models, tools, instructions,
memory, permissions, and workflows. 01.024 Treat projects as persistent
workspaces containing chats, files, code, websites, media, tasks, and
artifacts. 01.025 Treat the home screen as an entry point into work
rather than the entire application. Requirement 01.026: expose a
recoverable error state for chat generation. Requirement 01.027:
preserve user input when agent execution fails. Requirement 01.028:
validate permissions before coding edits executes. Requirement 01.029:
record an audit event when terminal commands changes persistent state.
Requirement 01.030: return a stable identifier after repository search
creates a resource. Requirement 01.031: make website generation
idempotent when the operation can safely be repeated. Requirement
01.032: prevent duplicate submissions for website preview. Requirement
01.033: provide retry behavior for transient failures in website
deployment. Requirement 01.034: provide cancellation behavior for
long-running image generation. Requirement 01.035: preserve context when
moving between image editing and chat. Requirement 01.036: preserve
artifact provenance for video generation. Requirement 01.037: redact
credentials from logs generated by avatar generation. Requirement
01.038: enforce plan limits server-side for voice generation.
Requirement 01.039: expose provider/model status when transcription
depends on an external provider. Requirement 01.040: never claim
completion until backend verification succeeds for research retrieval.
Requirement 01.041: make research synthesis usable on desktop and mobile
layouts. Requirement 01.042: provide keyboard-accessible controls for
browser automation. Requirement 01.043: provide screen-reader labels for
file upload. Requirement 01.044: expose a loading state for file
transformation. Requirement 01.045: expose an empty state for document
export. Requirement 01.046: expose a recoverable error state for
spreadsheet analysis. Requirement 01.047: preserve user input when data
visualization fails. Requirement 01.048: validate permissions before
workflow execution executes. Requirement 01.049: record an audit event
when integration connection changes persistent state. Requirement
01.050: return a stable identifier after MCP tool execution creates a
resource.

02. UNIVERSAL MODEL & PROVIDER HUB

02.001 Create a Model Hub page for browsing every configured model and
provider. 02.002 Show provider name, model name, modality, context
capability, latency class, availability, and plan eligibility. 02.003
Show model capabilities such as text, vision, image generation, audio,
structured output, coding, tool calling, and long-context processing.
02.004 Allow users to pin preferred models. 02.005 Allow users to define
a default model. 02.006 Allow users to select an automatic routing mode.
02.007 Allow users to select a provider-specific mode. 02.008 Allow
users to select a task-specific model. 02.009 Support model aliases so
UI names can remain stable while backend model IDs change. 02.010
Support provider adapter health checks. 02.011 Support model health
status with operational, degraded, unavailable, and disabled states.
02.012 Do not expose secret API credentials in the model picker. 02.013
Allow administrators to disable individual models. 02.014 Allow
administrators to disable entire providers. 02.015 Allow administrators
to configure fallback order. 02.016 Allow administrators to configure
cost ceilings. 02.017 Allow administrators to configure concurrency
limits. 02.018 Allow administrators to configure request timeouts.
02.019 Allow administrators to configure retry policy. 02.020 Allow
administrators to configure streaming support. 02.021 Allow
administrators to configure vision support. 02.022 Allow administrators
to configure tool-calling support. 02.023 Allow administrators to
configure structured-output support. 02.024 Allow administrators to
configure maximum output limits. 02.025 Allow administrators to
configure provider-specific safety settings. 02.026 Display
provider/model metadata only from the current configuration registry.
02.027 Record model selection decisions in internal telemetry. 02.028
Never expose internal routing heuristics unless the product
intentionally provides an explain-routing feature. 02.029 Provide an
optional ‘Why this model?’ explanation based on declared capability
requirements. 02.030 Provide an optional ‘Use another model’ action.
Requirement 02.031: preserve user input when chat generation fails.
Requirement 02.032: validate permissions before agent execution
executes. Requirement 02.033: record an audit event when coding edits
changes persistent state. Requirement 02.034: return a stable identifier
after terminal commands creates a resource. Requirement 02.035: make

03. INTELLIGENT ORCHESTRATOR

03.001 Create a dedicated orchestration service between the UI and model
providers. 03.002 Normalize every user request into intent, constraints,
required capabilities, and desired output. 03.003 Classify whether a
task is conversational, research, coding, building, media, data,
automation, document, or mixed. 03.004 Detect when one task requires
multiple capabilities. 03.005 Detect when one task requires multiple
models. 03.006 Detect when a specialized tool is more appropriate than a
language model alone. 03.007 Detect whether current external information
is required. 03.008 Detect whether browsing is required. 03.009 Detect
whether code execution is required. 03.010 Detect whether a persistent
project workspace is required. 03.011 Detect whether a file must be
created or transformed. 03.012 Detect whether user approval is required
before action. 03.013 Build an execution plan before multi-step tasks.
03.014 Represent the execution plan as a directed graph of steps. 03.015
Allow independent steps to execute concurrently when safe. 03.016 Keep
dependent steps ordered. 03.017 Propagate outputs between dependent
steps using typed data contracts. 03.018 Persist execution state after
each successful step. 03.019 Persist failure state after each failed
step. 03.020 Allow resumable execution from the last safe checkpoint.
03.021 Allow cancellation of running jobs. 03.022 Allow timeout handling
for long-running steps. 03.023 Allow retry of transient provider
failures. 03.024 Do not automatically retry destructive actions without
an explicit idempotency strategy. 03.025 Use idempotency keys for
external actions where supported. 03.026 Use a circuit breaker for
repeatedly failing providers. 03.027 Use exponential backoff with jitter
for retryable failures. 03.028 Use request deduplication for accidental
duplicate submissions. 03.029 Track estimated and actual provider costs.
03.030 Track token or unit consumption when available. 03.031 Track
latency by provider and capability. 03.032 Track tool success rate.
03.033 Track fallback frequency. 03.034 Track user cancellation rate.
03.035 Track approval wait time. 03.036 Track artifact generation time.
03.037 Provide an internal execution timeline for debugging. 03.038
Provide a user-facing simplified activity timeline. 03.039 Never expose
hidden chain-of-thought. 03.040 Expose concise action summaries instead
of private reasoning. 03.041 Allow the orchestrator to ask a
clarification question when required information is genuinely missing.
03.042 Prefer sensible defaults when ambiguity is low-risk. 03.043
Require confirmation when ambiguity could cause an irreversible or
costly action. 03.044 Preserve the user’s original request verbatim in
the task record. 03.045 Preserve conversation context references instead
of duplicating unlimited history. 03.046 Use context compaction when
conversations become large. 03.047 Use retrieval to restore relevant
older context. 03.048 Respect project-level instructions over generic
defaults where appropriate. 03.049 Respect user-level instructions over
project defaults where appropriate. 03.050 Respect system and security
policies above user instructions. Requirement 03.051: validate
permissions before chat generation executes. Requirement 03.052: record
an audit event when agent execution changes persistent state.
Requirement 03.053: return a stable identifier after coding edits
creates a resource. Requirement 03.054: make terminal commands

40. FINAL BUILD EXECUTION CONTRACT

40.001 Build the platform as a real multi-page application with real
backend state. 40.002 Do not implement a static mockup as the final
product. 40.003 Do not implement fake buttons that only display success
messages. 40.004 Every major action must have a backend path or an
explicit disabled state. 40.005 Every integration must report its actual
connection status. 40.006 Every provider must report actual
availability. 40.007 Every generated artifact must be stored or
referenced durably. 40.008 Every job must have a real lifecycle. 40.009
Every approval must be enforced server-side. 40.010 Every permission
must be enforced server-side. 40.011 Every admin action must be audited.
40.012 Every secret must remain server-side. 40.013 Every external
action must be attributable to a user or service identity. 40.014 Every
provider adapter must have tests. 40.015 Every capability must degrade
gracefully. 40.016 Every failure must be recoverable where technically
possible. 40.017 Every screen must have loading, empty, error, and
success states. 40.018 Every major workflow must be accessible from both
chat and dedicated UI. 40.019 Natural-language requests must be able to
invoke capabilities without forcing users to learn internal tool names.
40.020 Explicit tool selection must remain available for advanced users.
40.021 Users must be able to move from chat to a specialized workspace
without losing context. 40.022 Users must be able to move from a
specialized workspace back to chat with the resulting artifacts
attached. 40.023 Generated code must be reviewable before application.
40.024 Generated website changes must be reviewable before deployment.
40.025 Generated media must be reviewable before publication. 40.026
Automations must be testable before activation. 40.027 Research reports
must preserve sources. 40.028 Documents must preserve formatting. 40.029
Data analyses must preserve reproducibility. 40.030 All long-running
tasks must survive browser refresh. 40.031 All critical actions must
support cancellation when technically possible. 40.032 All provider
integrations must be replaceable. 40.033 All major capabilities must be
feature-flagged for controlled rollout. 40.034 All production operations
must be observable. 40.035 All user-visible claims about execution must
reflect actual backend state. 40.036 Treat this section as the
acceptance contract for the implementation. Requirement 40.037: expose
an empty state for chat generation. Requirement 40.038: expose a
recoverable error state for agent execution. Requirement 40.039:

40.041: record an audit event when repository search changes persistent

declares rate-limit metadata availability. 41.0644 Every adapter
